6080| Just an FYI for those not familiar with kickstarter. There's been a lot of trading card games on kickstarter that's burned people. By kickstarters terms, there is no guarantee you receive a product and no legal action you can take if you don't. Credit card companies will also deny charge backs to kickstarter. Hopefully doesn't happen here, but it happens a lot.

6126| Helios is cast in the Draw Phase. If neither player responds to the opportunity to respond in the draw phase then Helios cannot be cast until the next Draw Phase. If you pass priority and your opponent cast GG in **your** draw phase then you can respond with Helios because it is still the Draw Phase. It's best to cast Helios first regardless imo. You don't want to gamble that your opponent is going to do something in your Draw Phase if you don't.

6221| How do I request a refund?   If a project is still live, you can cancel your pledge before it ends. However, once a successful project has ended, and collection has started, it is no longer possible to cancel your pledge.  If you’re interested in having your collected pledge refunded, you’ll need to reach out to the creator directly. Keep in mind, however, that if the funds have already gone towards producing or shipping rewards then a refund may not be possible at this time. -------------------

6281| Nexus is moving spirits that already exist on the field to a different card.  Siphoning is pulling spirits on the field to fuel/pay the cost of an invoke/counter rune.  Two very different use cases.  Siphoning is simply another way to cast Invoke and Counter runes.  Nexus fuels a ton of gameplay strategy and sets up combos etc.

6313| Effects starting with "you can" are soft once per turn and only happen during your main phase (unless otherwise stated.) Effects starting with "when" can happen any amount of times per turn so long as the condition is met (this doesn't have to be in your main phase.) Lastly, effects starting with neither are passive effects which means they're always active unless the Elestral is suppressed (e.g. Tectaurus or Galaxea)

6437| Draft is a subset of a format called "Limited" where players construct their decks with a small selection of cards they obtain in the event itself.  Generally, the two most popular forms of limited play for TCGs are "Draft" and "Sealed".  Draft is where players open a pack of cards, choose one card out of that pack to add to their pool to construct their deck with, then pass the pack around in a circle. They continue to do so until all packs have been opened and players have a full pool of cards

6646| Proxying is using a stand-in in place of a card that represents said card.  It might be a random common with a piece of paper laid over top of it jammed into a sleeve, another card that was altered to be said card, a non-tournament legal version of a card or a slab of card stock that was intentionally made to replicate the card.  Typically proxying is frowned upon and illegal in tournament play. However when playing casually some people may be using proxies and will typically disclose they are d

7035| If both players Expend while their Spirit Decks are _empty_, then it's a tie.  If both players have a Spirit left, then they Expend it and continue playing. If either player believes they have a way to win from this game state, they can try to. If both players agree that they can't force the other to Expend again, then they can count their Main Decks as a shortcut; the player with fewer cards remaining loses.

7370| All good! Same logic applies, just tweaked for Runes. A Rune becomes successfully Cast when it begins resolving, so that would be when the Elestral would see the Rune as Cast. Runes don't begin resolving until the chain is closed, aka each player has passed priority back to back, and once the chain is closed you cannot add to it. So your Elestral's effect (that is looking for a Rune to be Cast) would add its effect to the chain building process of the next chain

7788| No, it's spindown dice. Rather than having the numbers arranged so the opposite sides of the dice lead to 21, the numbers are arranged so that each number is adjacent to the next in chronological order (i.e. 20 is adjacent to 19, 19 is adhacent to 18, etc.) This layout makes it not great for rolling but good for keeping track of life total or spirit count in this instance.

8134| Kindleo has two effects. The first effect is to Nexus up to <:Fire:1191467274029244538><:Fire:1191467274029244538>. The second effect is to target and destroy an Elestral, which triggers when the first effect resolves successfully. These are known as “linked conditional effects”. In between the first effect (Nexus) and second effect (destroy), there is a Field Check which sees that Kindleo is no longer Enchanted and must go to the Underworld.
